1️⃣7️⃣ Remove Duplicate Characters

Remove duplicate characters from a string.

Input:

programming

Output:

progamin
💡 Hint

Print a character only if it has not appeared earlier.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    char str[100];

    scanf("%99s", str);

    for(int i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
    {
        int duplicate = 0;

        for(int j = 0; j < i; j++)
        {
            if(str[i] == str[j])
            {
                duplicate = 1;
                break;
            }
        }

        if(!duplicate)
            printf("%c", str[i]);
    }

    return 0;
}

1️⃣8️⃣ First Non-Repeating Character

Find the first character that occurs only once.

Input:

aabbcdde

Output:

c
💡 Hint

Count the frequency of each character and return the first character with frequency 1.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    char str[100];

    scanf("%99s", str);

    for(int i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
    {
        int count = 0;

        for(int j = 0; str[j] != '\0'; j++)
        {
            if(str[i] == str[j])
                count++;
        }

        if(count == 1)
        {
            printf("%c", str[i]);
            return 0;
        }
    }

    printf("No non-repeating character");

    return 0;
}

1️⃣9️⃣ Check Anagram

Check whether two strings are anagrams.

Input:

listen
silent

Output:

Anagram
💡 Hint

Count the frequency of each character in both strings.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    char a[100], b[100];
    int countA[256] = {0};
    int countB[256] = {0};

    scanf("%99s", a);
    scanf("%99s", b);

    for(int i = 0; a[i] != '\0'; i++)
        countA[(unsigned char)a[i]]++;

    for(int i = 0; b[i] != '\0'; i++)
        countB[(unsigned char)b[i]]++;

    for(int i = 0; i < 256; i++)
    {
        if(countA[i] != countB[i])
        {
            printf("Not Anagram");
            return 0;
        }
    }

    printf("Anagram");

    return 0;
}

2️⃣0️⃣ Count Words in a Sentence

Count the number of words in a sentence.

Input:

C programming is easy

Output:

4
💡 Hint

Count transitions from whitespace to a non-whitespace character.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
#include <ctype.h>

int main()
{
    char str[200];
    int words = 0;
    int inWord = 0;

    fgets(str, sizeof(str), stdin);

    for(int i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
    {
        if(!isspace((unsigned char)str[i]))
        {
            if(!inWord)
            {
                words++;
                inWord = 1;
            }
        }
        else
        {
            inWord = 0;
        }
    }

    printf("%d", words);

    return 0;
}

4️⃣ Find Factorial Using Function

Write a function to find the factorial of a number.

Input:

5

Output:

120
💡 Hint

Multiply all numbers from 1 to n.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

long long factorial(int n)
{
    long long fact = 1;

    for(int i = 1; i <= n; i++)
        fact *= i;

    return fact;
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%lld", factorial(n));

    return 0;
}

5️⃣ Check Prime Using Function

Write a function to check whether a number is prime.

Input:

17

Output:

Prime
💡 Hint

Check divisibility from 2 up to sqrt(n).

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int isPrime(int n)
{
    if(n < 2)
        return 0;

    for(int i = 2; i * i <= n; i++)
    {
        if(n % i == 0)
            return 0;
    }

    return 1;
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    if(isPrime(n))
        printf("Prime");
    else
        printf("Not Prime");

    return 0;
}

6️⃣ Reverse Number Using Function

Write a function to reverse a number.

Input:

12345

Output:

54321
💡 Hint

Extract digits using % 10 and build the reversed number.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int reverseNumber(int n)
{
    int rev = 0;

    while(n != 0)
    {
        rev = rev * 10 + n % 10;
        n /= 10;
    }

    return rev;
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%d", reverseNumber(n));

    return 0;
}

7️⃣ Fibonacci Using Function

Write a function to print the first N Fibonacci numbers.

Input:

7

Output:

0 1 1 2 3 5 8
💡 Hint

Keep two variables for the previous two Fibonacci numbers.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void fibonacci(int n)
{
    int a = 0, b = 1;

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        printf("%d", a);

        if(i < n - 1)
            printf(" ");

        int c = a + b;
        a = b;
        b = c;
    }
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    fibonacci(n);

    return 0;
}

8️⃣ GCD Using Function

Find the greatest common divisor of two numbers.

Input:

24 36

Output:

12
💡 Hint

Use the Euclidean algorithm.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int gcd(int a, int b)
{
    while(b != 0)
    {
        int temp = b;
        b = a % b;
        a = temp;
    }

    return a;
}

int main()
{
    int a, b;

    scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);

    printf("%d", gcd(a, b));

    return 0;
}

9️⃣ Power Using Function

Calculate a raised to the power b using a function.

Input:

2 5

Output:

32
💡 Hint

Multiply the base by itself b times.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int power(int a, int b)
{
    int result = 1;

    for(int i = 1; i <= b; i++)
        result *= a;

    return result;
}

int main()
{
    int a, b;

    scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);

    printf("%d", power(a, b));

    return 0;
}

🔟 Factorial Using Recursion

Find the factorial of a number using recursion.

Input:

5

Output:

120
💡 Hint

The recursive relation is n! = n × (n-1)!.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

long long factorial(int n)
{
    if(n <= 1)
        return 1;

    return n * factorial(n - 1);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%lld", factorial(n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣1️⃣ Fibonacci Using Recursion

Find the Nth Fibonacci number using recursion.

Input:

6

Output:

8
💡 Hint

Use fib(n) = fib(n-1) + fib(n-2).

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int fibonacci(int n)
{
    if(n <= 1)
        return n;

    return fibonacci(n - 1) + fibonacci(n - 2);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%d", fibonacci(n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣2️⃣ Sum of Natural Numbers Using Recursion

Find the sum of the first N natural numbers using recursion.

Input:

10

Output:

55
💡 Hint

Use sum(n) = n + sum(n-1).

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int sum(int n)
{
    if(n == 0)
        return 0;

    return n + sum(n - 1);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%d", sum(n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣3️⃣ Sum of Digits Using Recursion

Find the sum of all digits of a number using recursion.

Input:

12345

Output:

15
💡 Hint

Take the last digit using % 10 and recursively process the remaining number.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int sumDigits(int n)
{
    if(n == 0)
        return 0;

    return n % 10 + sumDigits(n / 10);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%d", sumDigits(n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣4️⃣ Reverse a Number Using Recursion

Reverse a number using recursion.

Input:

1234

Output:

4321
💡 Hint

Use a helper function with a place-value multiplier.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int reverseHelper(int n, int rev)
{
    if(n == 0)
        return rev;

    return reverseHelper(n / 10,
                         rev * 10 + n % 10);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    printf("%d", reverseHelper(n, 0));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣5️⃣ Decimal to Binary Using Recursion

Convert a decimal number to binary using recursion.

Input:

10

Output:

1010
💡 Hint

Recursively divide the number by 2 and print the remainder while returning.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void binary(int n)
{
    if(n > 1)
        binary(n / 2);

    printf("%d", n % 2);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    binary(n);

    return 0;
}

1️⃣6️⃣ Find Maximum in Array Using Function

Write a function to find the largest element in an array.

Input:

5
10 25 7 40 18

Output:

40
💡 Hint

Pass the array and its size to the function.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int maximum(int arr[], int n)
{
    int max = arr[0];

    for(int i = 1; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(arr[i] > max)
            max = arr[i];
    }

    return max;
}

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    printf("%d", maximum(arr, n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣7️⃣ Count Even and Odd Using Function

Count the number of even and odd elements in an array.

Input:

6
1 2 3 4 5 6

Output:

Even: 3
Odd: 3
💡 Hint

Create separate functions for counting even and odd elements.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int countEven(int arr[], int n)
{
    int count = 0;

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(arr[i] % 2 == 0)
            count++;
    }

    return count;
}

int countOdd(int arr[], int n)
{
    int count = 0;

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(arr[i] % 2 != 0)
            count++;
    }

    return count;
}

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    printf("Even: %d\n", countEven(arr, n));
    printf("Odd: %d", countOdd(arr, n));

    return 0;
}

1️⃣8️⃣ Check Palindrome Using Function

Check whether a number is a palindrome using a function.

Input:

1221

Output:

Palindrome
💡 Hint

Reverse the number and compare it with the original.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int isPalindrome(int n)
{
    int original = n;
    int reverse = 0;

    while(n != 0)
    {
        reverse = reverse * 10 + n % 10;
        n /= 10;
    }

    return original == reverse;
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    if(isPalindrome(n))
        printf("Palindrome");
    else
        printf("Not Palindrome");

    return 0;
}

1️⃣9️⃣ LCM Using Function

Find the least common multiple of two numbers.

Input:

12 18

Output:

36
💡 Hint

Use the relationship LCM × GCD = a × b.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int gcd(int a, int b)
{
    while(b != 0)
    {
        int temp = b;
        b = a % b;
        a = temp;
    }

    return a;
}

int lcm(int a, int b)
{
    return (a / gcd(a, b)) * b;
}

int main()
{
    int a, b;

    scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);

    printf("%d", lcm(a, b));

    return 0;
}

2️⃣0️⃣ Tower of Hanoi

Solve the Tower of Hanoi problem using recursion.

Input:

3

Output:

Move disk 1 from A to C
Move disk 2 from A to B
Move disk 1 from C to B
Move disk 3 from A to C
Move disk 1 from B to A
Move disk 2 from B to C
Move disk 1 from A to C
💡 Hint

Move N-1 disks to the auxiliary rod, move the largest disk, then move N-1 disks to the destination rod.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void hanoi(int n, char source, char auxiliary, char destination)
{
    if(n == 1)
    {
        printf("Move disk 1 from %c to %c\n",
               source, destination);
        return;
    }

    hanoi(n - 1, source, destination, auxiliary);

    printf("Move disk %d from %c to %c\n",
           n, source, destination);

    hanoi(n - 1, auxiliary, source, destination);
}

int main()
{
    int n;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    hanoi(n, 'A', 'B', 'C');

    return 0;
}

3️⃣ Swap Two Numbers Using Pointers

Swap two numbers using a function and pointers.

Input:

10 20

Output:

20 10
💡 Hint

Pass the addresses of both variables to the function.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void swap(int *a, int *b)
{
    int temp = *a;

    *a = *b;
    *b = temp;
}

int main()
{
    int a, b;

    scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);

    swap(&a, &b);

    printf("%d %d", a, b);

    return 0;
}

4️⃣ Sum Array Using Pointer

Find the sum of array elements using pointer arithmetic.

Input:

5
10 20 30 40 50

Output:

150
💡 Hint

Access elements using *(p + i).

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];
    int *p = arr;
    int sum = 0;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        sum += *(p + i);

    printf("%d", sum);

    return 0;
}

5️⃣ Find Maximum Using Pointer

Find the largest element in an array using pointers.

Input:

5
12 45 7 30 20

Output:

45
💡 Hint

Compare values obtained through pointer arithmetic.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];
    int *p;
    int max;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    p = arr;
    max = *p;

    for(int i = 1; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(*(p + i) > max)
            max = *(p + i);
    }

    printf("%d", max);

    return 0;
}

6️⃣ Reverse Array Using Pointers

Reverse an array using pointers.

Input:

5
1 2 3 4 5

Output:

5 4 3 2 1
💡 Hint

Use two pointers: one at the beginning and one at the end of the array.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    int *left = arr;
    int *right = arr + n - 1;

    while(left < right)
    {
        int temp = *left;

        *left = *right;
        *right = temp;

        left++;
        right--;
    }

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        printf("%d", arr[i]);

        if(i < n - 1)
            printf(" ");
    }

    return 0;
}

7️⃣ Count Array Elements Using Pointers

Count the number of elements in an array using pointers.

Input:

6
10 20 30 40 50 60

Output:

6
💡 Hint

Move a pointer from the first element until the end pointer is reached.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    int *start = arr;
    int *end = arr + n;

    printf("%ld", end - start);

    return 0;
}

8️⃣ Dynamically Allocate an Array

Allocate memory dynamically for N integers and print their sum.

Input:

5
10 20 30 40 50

Output:

150
💡 Hint

Use malloc() to allocate memory and free() after use.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

int main()
{
    int n;
    int *arr;
    int sum = 0;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    arr = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

    if(arr == NULL)
        return 1;

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);
        sum += arr[i];
    }

    printf("%d", sum);

    free(arr);

    return 0;
}

1️⃣1️⃣ Resize Array Using realloc()

Create an array of N elements and increase its size to M elements using realloc().

Input:

3
10 20 30
5
40 50

Output:

10 20 30 40 50
💡 Hint

Use realloc() to increase the allocated memory.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

int main()
{
    int n, m;
    int *arr;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    arr = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

    if(arr == NULL)
        return 1;

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    scanf("%d", &m);

    arr = realloc(arr, m * sizeof(int));

    if(arr == NULL)
        return 1;

    for(int i = n; i < m; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    for(int i = 0; i < m; i++)
    {
        printf("%d", arr[i]);

        if(i < m - 1)
            printf(" ");
    }

    free(arr);

    return 0;
}

1️⃣2️⃣ Find Sum Using Pointer Function

50

#include <stdio.h>

int sum(int *arr, int n)

    int total = 0;

        total += *(arr + i);

    return total;
}

{
    int n, arr[100];

        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

1️⃣3️⃣ Swap Array Elements Using Pointers

Swap two elements of an array using pointers.

10 50 30 40 20
💡 Hint

Pass the addresses of the two selected elements.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void swap(int *a, int *b)
{
    int temp = *a;

    *a = *b;
    *b = temp;
}

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];
    int x, y;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        printf("%d", arr[i]);

        if(i < n - 1)
            printf(" ");
    }

    return 0;
}

1️⃣4️⃣ Find String Length Using Pointer

5

{
    char str[100];
    char *p;
    int length = 0;

    scanf("%99s", str);

    p = str;

    while(*p != '\0')
    {
        length++;
        p++;
    }

    return 0;
}

1️⃣5️⃣ Reverse String Using Pointer

Reverse a string using pointers.

Input:

hello

Output:

olleh
💡 Hint

Find the last character and move the pointer backwards.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    char str[100];
    char *p;
    int length = 0;

    scanf("%99s", str);

    while(str[length] != '\0')
        length++;

    p = str + length - 1;

    while(p >= str)
    {
        printf("%c", *p);
        p--;
    }

    return 0;
}

1️⃣9️⃣ Dynamic Matrix

Dynamically allocate memory for a matrix and print it.

Input:

2 3
1 2 3
4 5 6

Output:

1 2 3
4 5 6
💡 Hint

Allocate memory for rows and then allocate memory for each row.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>

int main()
{
    int rows, cols;
    int **matrix;

    scanf("%d %d", &rows, &cols);

    matrix = malloc(rows * sizeof(int *));

    if(matrix == NULL)
        return 1;

    for(int i = 0; i < rows; i++)
        matrix[i] = malloc(cols * sizeof(int));

    for(int i = 0; i < rows; i++)
    {
        for(int j = 0; j < cols; j++)
            scanf("%d", &matrix[i][j]);
    }

    for(int i = 0; i < rows; i++)
    {
        for(int j = 0; j < cols; j++)
        {
            printf("%d", matrix[i][j]);

            if(j < cols - 1)
                printf(" ");
        }

        printf("\n");
    }

    for(int i = 0; i < rows; i++)
        free(matrix[i]);

    free(matrix);

    return 0;
}

2️⃣0️⃣ Find Second Largest Using Pointers

Find the second largest element using pointer arithmetic.

Input:

5
10 40 20 50 30

Output:

40
💡 Hint

Maintain the largest and second largest values while traversing the array.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
#include <limits.h>

int main()
{
    int n, arr[100];
    int largest = INT_MIN;
    int second = INT_MIN;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        scanf("%d", &arr[i]);

    for(int *p = arr; p < arr + n; p++)
    {
        if(*p > largest)
        {
            second = largest;
            largest = *p;
        }
        else if(*p > second && *p != largest)
        {
            second = *p;
        }
    }

    printf("%d", second);

    return 0;
}

1️⃣ Store Student Details Using Structure

Create a structure to store a student's roll number, name and marks.

Input:

101
Ravi
85

Output:

101 Ravi 85
💡 Hint

Create a structure containing roll number, name and marks.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    float marks;
};

int main()
{
    struct Student s;

    scanf("%d", &s.roll);
    scanf("%49s", s.name);

    printf("%d %s %.0f",
           s.roll, s.name, s.marks);

2️⃣ Find Student with Highest Marks

Store details of N students and find the student with the highest marks.

Input:

3
101 Ravi 80
102 Sita 92
103 Arun 85

Output:

Sita 92
💡 Hint

Use an array of structures and keep track of the highest marks.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    int marks;
};

int main()
{
    int n;
    struct Student s[100];
    int maxIndex = 0;

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        scanf("%d %49s %d",
              &s[i].roll,
              s[i].name,
              &s[i].marks);
    }

    for(int i = 1; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(s[i].marks > s[maxIndex].marks)
            maxIndex = i;
    }

    printf("%s %d",
           s[maxIndex].name,
           s[maxIndex].marks);

    return 0;
}

101 Ravi 85

{

6️⃣ Search Student by Roll Number

Search for a student using the roll number.

Input:

3
101 Ravi 80
102 Sita 90
103 Arun 85
102

Output:

Sita 90
💡 Hint

Traverse the array of structures and compare roll numbers.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    int marks;
};

int main()
{
    int n, search;
    struct Student s[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        scanf("%d %49s %d",
              &s[i].roll,
              s[i].name,
              &s[i].marks);
    }

    scanf("%d", &search);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        if(s[i].roll == search)
        {
            printf("%s %d",
                   s[i].name,
                   s[i].marks);
            return 0;
        }
    }

    printf("Student Not Found");

    return 0;
}

7️⃣ Sort Students by Marks

Sort student records according to marks in descending order.

Input:

3
101 Ravi 70
102 Sita 90
103 Arun 80

Output:

Sita 90
Arun 80
Ravi 70
💡 Hint

Use selection sort or bubble sort on the structure array.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    int marks;
};

int main()
{
    int n;
    struct Student s[100];

    scanf("%d", &n);

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    {
        scanf("%d %49s %d",
              &s[i].roll,
              s[i].name,
              &s[i].marks);
    }

    for(int i = 0; i < n - 1; i++)
    {
        for(int j = i + 1; j < n; j++)
        {
            if(s[i].marks < s[j].marks)
            {
                struct Student temp = s[i];
                s[i] = s[j];
                s[j] = temp;
            }
        }
    }

    for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
        printf("%s %d\n", s[i].name, s[i].marks);

    return 0;
}

8️⃣ Nested Structure

Create a student structure containing a nested address structure.

Input:

101
Ravi
Hyderabad

Output:

101 Ravi Hyderabad
💡 Hint

Define one structure inside another structure.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Address
{
    char city[50];
};

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    struct Address address;
};

int main()
{
    struct Student s;

    scanf("%d", &s.roll);
    scanf("%49s", s.name);
    scanf("%49s", s.address.city);

    printf("%d %s %s",
           s.roll,
           s.name,
           s.address.city);

    return 0;
}

9️⃣ Union Memory Concept

Create a union containing an integer, float and character. Store one value and display it.

Input:

25

Output:

25
💡 Hint

Union members share the same memory location. Only the most recently stored member should be read.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

union Data
{
    int i;
    float f;
    char c;
};

int main()
{
    union Data d;

    scanf("%d", &d.i);

    printf("%d", d.i);

    return 0;
}

{
    FILE *fp;

    fp = fopen("data.txt", "r");

1️⃣3️⃣ Count Lines in a File

Count the number of lines in a text file.

Input:

data.txt:
Line 1
Line 2
Line 3

Output:

3
💡 Hint

Count newline characters. Also handle a final line that does not end with a newline.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    FILE *fp;
    int ch;
    int lines = 0;
    int last = '\n';

    fp = fopen("data.txt", "r");

    if(fp == NULL)
        return 1;

    while((ch = fgetc(fp)) != EOF)
    {
        if(ch == '\n')
            lines++;

        last = ch;
    }

    if(last != '\n')
        lines++;

    fclose(fp);

    printf("%d", lines);

    return 0;
}

1️⃣4️⃣ Copy One File to Another

Copy the contents of one file into another file.

Input:

source.txt:
Hello C

Output:

destination.txt:
Hello C
💡 Hint

Open the source in read mode and destination in write mode. Copy characters one by one.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
    FILE *source, *destination;
    int ch;

    source = fopen("source.txt", "r");
    destination = fopen("destination.txt", "w");

    if(source == NULL || destination == NULL)
        return 1;

    while((ch = fgetc(source)) != EOF)
        fputc(ch, destination);

    fclose(source);
    fclose(destination);

    printf("File copied successfully");

    return 0;
}

1️⃣9️⃣ Binary File Using Structure

Store a structure in a binary file using fwrite().

Input:

101 Ravi 85

Output:

Record stored successfully
💡 Hint

Use fwrite() to write the entire structure into the binary file.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    int marks;
};

int main()
{
    FILE *fp;
    struct Student s;

    scanf("%d %49s %d",
          &s.roll,
          s.name,
          &s.marks);

    fp = fopen("student.dat", "wb");

    if(fp == NULL)
        return 1;

    fwrite(&s, sizeof(s), 1, fp);

    fclose(fp);

    printf("Record stored successfully");

    return 0;
}

2️⃣0️⃣ Student Management Mini Project

Create a menu-driven student management system using structures and file handling.

The program should support:

  • Add student
  • Display students
  • Search student
  • Update student
  • Delete student
  • Exit

Input:

1
101
Ravi
85

Output:

Student added successfully
💡 Hint

Use a structure for student information, functions for each operation and a file to permanently store the records.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

struct Student
{
    int roll;
    char name[50];
    int marks;
};

void addStudent()
{
    FILE *fp;
    struct Student s;

    fp = fopen("students.dat", "ab");

    if(fp == NULL)
    {
        printf("File Error\n");
        return;
    }

    printf("Enter Roll Number: ");
    scanf("%d", &s.roll);

    printf("Enter Name: ");
    scanf("%49s", s.name);

    printf("Enter Marks: ");
    scanf("%d", &s.marks);

    fwrite(&s, sizeof(s), 1, fp);

    fclose(fp);

    printf("Student added successfully\n");
}

void displayStudents()
{
    FILE *fp;
    struct Student s;

    fp = fopen("students.dat", "rb");

    if(fp == NULL)
    {
        printf("No records found\n");
        return;
    }

    while(fread(&s, sizeof(s), 1, fp) == 1)
    {
        printf("Roll: %d\n", s.roll);
        printf("Name: %s\n", s.name);
        printf("Marks: %d\n\n", s.marks);
    }

    fclose(fp);
}

int main()
{
    int choice;

    do
    {
        printf("1. Add Student\n");
        printf("2. Display Students\n");
        printf("3. Exit\n");

        scanf("%d", &choice);

        switch(choice)
        {
            case 1:
                addStudent();
                break;

            case 2:
                displayStudents();
                break;

            case 3:
                printf("Exiting...");
                break;

            default:
                printf("Invalid choice\n");
        }

    } while(choice != 3);

    return 0;
}

5️⃣ Count Words in a File

Count the number of words present in a text file.

Input:

data.txt:

C programming is easy

Output:

4
💡 Hint

Track whether you are currently inside a word. Use whitespace to identify word boundaries.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
FILE *fp;
int ch;
int words = 0;
int inWord = 0;

fp = fopen("data.txt", "r");

if(fp == NULL)
{
    printf("File not found");
    return 0;
}

while((ch = fgetc(fp)) != EOF)
{
    if(isspace(ch))
    {
        inWord = 0;
    }
    else if(!inWord)
    {
        words++;
        inWord = 1;
    }
}

fclose(fp);

printf("%d", words);

return 0;

}

C programming is powerful

programming

Found
💡 Hint

Read words using fscanf() and compare each word with the search word using strcmp().

#include 

int main()
{

char word[100];
char search[100];

if(fp == NULL)
{
    printf("File not found");
    return 0;
}

}

printf("Not Found");

return 0;

}

1️⃣2️⃣ Allocate Memory Using malloc()

Dynamically allocate memory for N integers using malloc() and print them.

Input:

5

10 20 30 40 50

Output:

10 20 30 40 50
💡 Hint

Use malloc(n * sizeof(int)) to allocate memory and release it using free().

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

int *a = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

if(a == NULL)
{
    printf("Memory allocation failed");
    return 0;
}

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    printf("%d ", a[i]);

free(a);

return 0;

}

1️⃣5️⃣ Resize Memory Using realloc()

Allocate memory for N integers and increase the array size to M using realloc().

Input:

3

10 20 30
5
40 50

Output:

10 20 30 40 50
💡 Hint

First allocate N integers using malloc(). Then use realloc() to increase the size to M.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
int n, m;

scanf("%d", &n);

int *a = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

if(a == NULL)
    return 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

scanf("%d", &m);

int *temp = realloc(a, m * sizeof(int));

if(temp == NULL)
{
    free(a);
    return 0;
}

a = temp;

for(int i = n; i < m; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < m; i++)
    printf("%d ", a[i]);

free(a);

return 0;

}

1️⃣6️⃣ Find Largest Element Using Dynamic Memory

Dynamically allocate an array and find its largest element.

Input:

5

10 45 20 67 30

Output:

67
💡 Hint

Store the values in dynamically allocated memory and maintain the maximum value while traversing.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

int *a = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

if(a == NULL)
    return 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

int max = a[0];

for(int i = 1; i < n; i++)
{
    if(a[i] > max)
        max = a[i];
}

printf("%d", max);

free(a);

return 0;

}

1️⃣7️⃣ Reverse Dynamically Allocated Array

Dynamically allocate an array and reverse its elements.

Input:

5

10 20 30 40 50

Output:

50 40 30 20 10
💡 Hint

Use two indexes: one from the beginning and one from the end.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

int *a = malloc(n * sizeof(int));

if(a == NULL)
    return 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

int start = 0;
int end = n - 1;

while(start < end)
{
    int temp = a[start];
    a[start] = a[end];
    a[end] = temp;

    start++;
    end--;
}

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    printf("%d ", a[i]);

free(a);

return 0;

}

1️⃣8️⃣ Dynamically Allocate a String

Dynamically allocate memory for a string and display the entered string.

Input:

Programming

Output:

Programming
💡 Hint

Allocate enough memory for the string and its null character using malloc().

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 
#include 

int main()
{
char temp[100];

fgets(temp, sizeof(temp), stdin);

temp[strcspn(temp, "\n")] = '\0';

char *str = malloc((strlen(temp) + 1) * sizeof(char));

if(str == NULL)
    return 0;

strcpy(str, temp);

printf("%s", str);

free(str);

return 0;

}

1️⃣9️⃣ Dynamic Memory with Structure

Dynamically allocate memory for N students using structures and display their details.

Input:

2

Ravi 85
Sita 92

Output:

Ravi 85

Sita 92
💡 Hint

Use malloc() with sizeof(struct Student) to create an array of structures dynamically.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

struct Student
{
char name[50];
int marks;
};

int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

struct Student *s =
    malloc(n * sizeof(struct Student));

if(s == NULL)
    return 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    scanf("%49s %d", s[i].name, &s[i].marks);
}

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    printf("%s %d\n",
           s[i].name,
           s[i].marks);
}

free(s);

return 0;


}

2️⃣0️⃣ Student Records Using File and Dynamic Memory

Dynamically allocate memory for student records, store them in a file and then read and display them.

Input:

2


Ravi 85
Sita 92

Output:

Ravi 85


Sita 92
💡 Hint

Allocate the student array using malloc(). Write the records using fprintf(), then reopen the file and read them using fscanf().

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


#include 

struct Student
{
char name[50];
int marks;
};

int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

struct Student *s =
    malloc(n * sizeof(struct Student));

if(s == NULL)
    return 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    scanf("%49s %d",
          s[i].name,
          &s[i].marks);
}

FILE *fp = fopen("students.txt", "w");

if(fp == NULL)
{
    free(s);
    return 0;
}

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    fprintf(fp, "%s %d\n",
            s[i].name,
            s[i].marks);
}

fclose(fp);

free(s);

fp = fopen("students.txt", "r");

if(fp == NULL)
    return 0;

struct Student temp;

while(fscanf(fp, "%49s %d",
             temp.name,
             &temp.marks) == 2)
{
    printf("%s %d\n",
           temp.name,
           temp.marks);
}

fclose(fp);

return 0;


}

13

3

{

while(n > 0)
{
    count += n & 1;
    n = n >> 1;
}

return 0;


}

{

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    int x;
    scanf("%d", &x);

    result = result ^ x;
}

return 0;


}

2️⃣0️⃣ Reverse Bits of an 8-bit Number

Reverse the bits of an 8-bit unsigned number.

Input:

13

Output:

176
💡 Hint

Process 8 bits one at a time. Take the last bit of the input and append it to the result from the left.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
unsigned int n;
unsigned int result = 0;

scanf("%u", &n);

for(int i = 0; i < 8; i++)
{
    result = (result << 1) | (n & 1);
    n = n >> 1;
}

printf("%u", result);

return 0;


}

1️⃣ Second Largest Element

Find the second largest element in an array without sorting it.

Input:

6

10 25 8 40 15 30

Output:

30
💡 Hint

Maintain both the largest and second largest values while traversing the array.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

int largest = INT_MIN;
int second = INT_MIN;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    if(a[i] > largest)
    {
        second = largest;
        largest = a[i];
    }
    else if(a[i] > second && a[i] != largest)
    {
        second = a[i];
    }
}

printf("%d", second);

return 0;


}

2️⃣ Remove Duplicates from Array

Remove duplicate elements from an array and print only the unique elements.

Input:

7

10 20 10 30 20 40 30

Output:

10 20 30 40
💡 Hint

For each element, check whether it has already appeared before.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    int unique = 1;

    for(int j = 0; j < i; j++)
    {
        if(a[i] == a[j])
        {
            unique = 0;
            break;
        }
    }

    if(unique)
        printf("%d ", a[i]);
}

return 0;


}

3️⃣ Move All Zeros to End

Move all zero elements to the end while maintaining the order of non-zero elements.

Input:

7


0 1 0 3 12 0 5

Output:

1 3 12 5 0 0 0
💡 Hint

Keep a position for the next non-zero element.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>
            
int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

int pos = 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    if(a[i] != 0)
        a[pos++] = a[i];
}

while(pos < n)
    a[pos++] = 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    printf("%d ", a[i]);

return 0;

}

4️⃣ Find Missing Number

An array contains numbers from 1 to N with one number missing. Find the missing number.

Input:

5

1 2 4 5

Output:

3
💡 Hint

Calculate the expected sum from 1 to N and subtract the array sum.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int sum = n * (n + 1) / 2;
int actual = 0;

for(int i = 0; i < n - 1; i++)
{
    int x;
    scanf("%d", &x);
    actual += x;
}

printf("%d", sum - actual);

return 0;

}

5️⃣ Maximum Subarray Sum

Find the maximum possible sum of a contiguous subarray.

Input:

8

-2 1 -3 4 -1 2 1 -5

Output:

6
💡 Hint

Use Kadane's algorithm. At each position decide whether to start a new subarray or continue the current one.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int x;
scanf("%d", &x);

int current = x;
int maximum = x;

for(int i = 1; i < n; i++)
{
    scanf("%d", &x);

    if(current + x > x)
        current = current + x;
    else
        current = x;

    if(current > maximum)
        maximum = current;
}

printf("%d", maximum);

return 0;

}

6️⃣ Rotate Array Right by K Positions

Rotate an array to the right by K positions.

Input:

5 2

1 2 3 4 5

Output:

4 5 1 2 3
💡 Hint

Use the reversal technique or repeatedly shift elements. Remember to reduce K using K % N.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

void reverse(int a[], int left, int right)
{
while(left < right)
{
int temp = a[left];
a[left] = a[right];
a[right] = temp;

    left++;
    right--;
}

}

int main()
{
int n, k;

scanf("%d %d", &n, &k);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

k = k % n;

reverse(a, 0, n - 1);
reverse(a, 0, k - 1);
reverse(a, k, n - 1);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    printf("%d ", a[i]);

return 0;

}

7️⃣ First Non-Repeating Character

Find the first character that appears only once in a string.

Input:

swiss

Output:

w
💡 Hint

Count the frequency of every character and then scan the string again.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
char str[100];
int freq[256] = {0};

scanf("%99s", str);

for(int i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
    freq[(unsigned char)str[i]]++;

for(int i = 0; str[i] != '\0'; i++)
{
    if(freq[(unsigned char)str[i]] == 1)
    {
        printf("%c", str[i]);
        return 0;
    }
}

printf("No unique character");

return 0;

}

8️⃣ Check Anagram

Check whether two strings are anagrams of each other.

Input:

listen

silent

Output:

Anagram
💡 Hint

Compare the frequency of every character in both strings.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
char a[100], b[100];
int freq[256] = {0};

scanf("%99s", a);
scanf("%99s", b);

if(strlen(a) != strlen(b))
{
    printf("Not Anagram");
    return 0;
}

for(int i = 0; a[i] != '\0'; i++)
{
    freq[(unsigned char)a[i]]++;
    freq[(unsigned char)b[i]]--;
}

for(int i = 0; i < 256; i++)
{
    if(freq[i] != 0)
    {
        printf("Not Anagram");
        return 0;
    }
}

printf("Anagram");

return 0;

}

9️⃣ Reverse Words in a String

Reverse the order of words in a sentence.

Input:

C is powerful

Output:

powerful is C
💡 Hint

Read the sentence and print words from the last word to the first.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

#include 

int main()
{
char str[200];
char words[50][50];
int count = 0;

fgets(str, sizeof(str), stdin);

char *token = strtok(str, " \n");

while(token != NULL)
{
    strcpy(words[count++], token);
    token = strtok(NULL, " \n");
}

for(int i = count - 1; i >= 0; i--)
    printf("%s ", words[i]);

return 0;

}

🔟 Check Palindrome Number

Check whether a number is a palindrome.

Input:

1221

Output:

Palindrome
💡 Hint

Reverse the number and compare it with the original.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
int n, original, reverse = 0;

scanf("%d", &n);

original = n;

while(n > 0)
{
    reverse = reverse * 10 + n % 10;
    n /= 10;
}

if(original == reverse)
    printf("Palindrome");
else
    printf("Not Palindrome");

return 0;


}

1️⃣1️⃣ Armstrong Number

Check whether a three-digit number is an Armstrong number.

Input:

153

Output:

Armstrong
💡 Hint

For a three-digit number, calculate the sum of cubes of its digits.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n, original, sum = 0;

scanf("%d", &n);

original = n;

while(n > 0)
{
    int digit = n % 10;
    sum += digit * digit * digit;
    n /= 10;
}

if(sum == original)
    printf("Armstrong");
else
    printf("Not Armstrong");

return 0;


}

1️⃣2️⃣ Prime Numbers in a Range

Print all prime numbers between L and R.

Input:

10 20

Output:

11 13 17 19
💡 Hint

For every number, check divisibility only up to its square root.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int isPrime(int n)
{
if(n < 2)
return 0;


for(int i = 2; i * i <= n; i++)
{
    if(n % i == 0)
        return 0;
}

return 1;


}

int main()
{
int l, r;


scanf("%d %d", &l, &r);

for(int i = l; i <= r; i++)
{
    if(isPrime(i))
        printf("%d ", i);
}

return 0;

}

1️⃣3️⃣ GCD of Two Numbers

Find the greatest common divisor of two integers.

Input:

48 18

Output:

6
💡 Hint

Use Euclid's algorithm: repeatedly replace the larger number using the remainder.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int a, b;

scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);

while(b != 0)
{
    int temp = b;
    b = a % b;
    a = temp;
}

printf("%d", a);

return 0;

}

1️⃣4️⃣ Decimal to Binary

Convert a decimal number to binary without using library conversion functions.

Input:

13

Output:

1101
💡 Hint

Repeatedly divide by 2 and store the remainders. Print them in reverse order.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n;
int binary[32];
int i = 0;

scanf("%d", &n);

if(n == 0)
{
    printf("0");
    return 0;
}

while(n > 0)
{
    binary[i++] = n % 2;
    n /= 2;
}

while(i > 0)
    printf("%d", binary[--i]);

return 0;


}

1️⃣5️⃣ Count Set Bits

Count the number of set bits in a positive integer.

29

Output:

4
💡 Hint

Use n & (n - 1) to remove the lowest set bit during every iteration.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
unsigned int n;
int count = 0;

scanf("%u", &n);

while(n)
{
    n = n & (n - 1);
    count++;
}

return 0;


}

1️⃣6️⃣ Find Duplicate Number

An array contains numbers from 1 to N-1 and exactly one number appears twice. Find the duplicate.

Input:

5


1 3 4 2 3

Output:

3
💡 Hint

Compare every pair of elements or use a frequency based approach. Focus first on correctness.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n;


scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    for(int j = i + 1; j < n; j++)
    {
        if(a[i] == a[j])
        {
            printf("%d", a[i]);
            return 0;
        }
    }
}

return 0;


}

1️⃣7️⃣ Find Pair with Given Sum

Find whether two elements in an array add up to a given target value.

Input:

5 9


2 7 11 15 1

Output:

2 7
💡 Hint

Check every pair and compare its sum with the target.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n, target;

scanf("%d %d", &n, &target);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    for(int j = i + 1; j < n; j++)
    {
        if(a[i] + a[j] == target)
        {
            printf("%d %d", a[i], a[j]);
            return 0;
        }
    }
}

printf("No Pair");

return 0;


}

1️⃣8️⃣ Frequency of Array Elements

Print the frequency of every distinct element in an array.

Input:

6


2 3 2 4 3 2

Output:

2 - 3


3 - 2
4 - 1
💡 Hint

For each element, count its occurrences and make sure you don't print the same element twice.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n;


scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    int already = 0;

    for(int j = 0; j < i; j++)
    {
        if(a[i] == a[j])
        {
            already = 1;
            break;
        }
    }

    if(already)
        continue;

    int count = 0;

    for(int j = 0; j < n; j++)
    {
        if(a[i] == a[j])
            count++;
    }

    printf("%d - %d\n", a[i], count);
}

return 0;


}

1️⃣9️⃣ Sort 0s, 1s and 2s

Sort an array containing only 0, 1 and 2 without using a general sorting algorithm.

Input:

6


2 0 2 1 1 0

Output:

0 0 1 1 2 2
💡 Hint

Count the number of 0s, 1s and 2s, then rebuild the array.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>

int main()
{
int n;
scanf("%d", &n);

int count[3] = {0};

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
{
    int x;
    scanf("%d", &x);

    if(x >= 0 && x <= 2)
        count[x]++;
}

for(int i = 0; i < 3; i++)
{
    for(int j = 0; j < count[i]; j++)
        printf("%d ", i);
}

return 0;


}

2️⃣0️⃣ ⭐ Final Placement Challenge — Leaders in Array

An element is called a leader if it is greater than every element to its right. Print all leaders.

Input:

6


16 17 4 3 5 2

Output:

17 5 2
💡 Hint

Start from the right side. Maintain the maximum value seen so far.

✅ Solution 
#include <stdio.h>


int main()
{
int n;

scanf("%d", &n);

int a[n];

for(int i = 0; i < n; i++)
    scanf("%d", &a[i]);

int leaders[n];
int count = 0;

int maximum = a[n - 1];

leaders[count++] = maximum;

for(int i = n - 2; i >= 0; i--)
{
    if(a[i] > maximum)
    {
        maximum = a[i];
        leaders[count++] = a[i];
    }
}

for(int i = count - 1; i >= 0; i--)
    printf("%d ", leaders[i]);

return 0;


}
